RAJYA SABHA 
Friday, the 28th June, 2024 / 7 Ashadha, 1946 (Saka) 
The House met at eleven of the clock, 
MR. CHAIRMAN in the Chair. 
BIRTHDAY GREETINGS 
MR. CHAIRMAN:   Hon. Members, I am pleased to extend birthday greetings to hon. 
Member of Parliament, Prof. Ram Gopal Yadav. His birthday  is tomorrow, 29th June. 
It is always good to wish in advance rather than later.   
A fifth term Member of Rajya Sabha, since 1992, Prof. Yadav also served as a 
Member of Lok Sabha from 2004 to 2008.  A grassroot politicia n, Prof. Yadav is 
currently General Secretary and the floor leader of Samajwadi Party.   
Prof. Ram Gopal Yadav was twice part of Indian Parliamentary delegation to 
United Nations in 2011 and 2015.  His address in the United Nations General Assembly 
in 2015 was impactful and widely appreciated.  Prof. Yadav, an educationist,  has 
authored several books.  His book "Dr. Lohiya Ka Samajwad" is a treatise to be read 
and his another book "Sansad Main Meri Baat" lays bare his heart. Prof. Yadav is a 
gifted man with captivating smile and amiable temperament. He exemplifies 
parliamentary decorum worth emulating.   
He is blessed with two sons, Akshay  and Anupam, and daughter Anuradha. 
His son, Akshay Yadav, has recently been elected to the Lok Sa bha and this is the 
second term of Akshay in Lok Sabha. Hon. Members, on behalf of this House, I wish 
him a long, healthy and happy life and extend  greetings to his family and friends. I 
have been captivated by his smile a number of  times. I am sure that during Session 
and further Sessions, he would spare me.   
Hon. Members, I am pleased to extend my birthday greetings to Shri 
Harivansh, hon. Deputy Chairperson. His birthday is on 30 th June.  Shri Harivansh, 
erudite and scholarly, has been a journalist of eminence committed to ethics and 
public cause. He also served as Additional  Media Advisor to former Prime Minister 
Shri Chandrashekha, when I was a Member of Ninth Lok Sabha. His journalistic sense 
smelt scams that have contributed to  public accountability. His impactful 
contributions, while being part of parlia mentary delegations abroad, have made us 
proud.  He espoused the stance of the nati on with dignity and firmness, premised on 

factful foundations.  As Ch airman, Rajya Sabha, I am fortunate to be enormously 
benefited from his wide experience in pu blic life and counsel in taking several 
initiatives and innovative steps in Rajya Sabha, including human resource capacity 
building, Rajya Sabha Fellowships and Intern ship Programmes as also digitization.   
He is blessed with son - Vikas Singh, daughter - Richa Singh and his talented 
spouse, Shrimati Asha -- Asha of his life-- is his spinal strength.  His life is 
exemplified by simplicity, amiability, receptivity and dignity.   
 Hon. Members, on your and my behalf, I wish him a long, healthy, happy life 
and extend greetings to his family members.  
 
  
OBITUARY REFERENCES 
 
MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon. Members, I refer with profound sorrow to the passing away of 
former Members of this House, Dr. Ram Prak ash, Shri Fali S. Nariman, Shri Sushil 
Kumar Modi and Shri Murlidhar Chandrakant Bhandare.  
 Dr. Ram Prakash passed away on the 13
th of December, 2023.  He served as a 
Member of this House representing the St ate of Haryana for two consecutive terms, 
f r o m  M a r c h ,  2 0 0 7  t o  A p r i l ,  2 0 1 4 .   H e  a l s o  s e r v e d  a s  a  M e m b e r  o f  t h e  H a r y a n a  
Legislative Assembly and as a Cabinet Minist er in the Government of Haryana holding 
the Science, Technology, and Electronics portfolios. 
 Dr. Ram Prakash, a Professor of Chemistry, remained associated with several 
educational institutes in different capacities and worked assiduously to build public 
opinion against female foeticide. 
 In his passing away, the country has lost  an eminent educationist, a dedicated 
social worker, and an able parliamentarian.  
 Shri Fali S. Nariman , a renowned legal luminary, a jurist, passed away on the 
21
st of February, 2024.  He served as a No minated Member of this House, from 
November, 1999 to November, 2005.   
 Born on 10
th January, 1929, at Rangoon in erstwhile undivided India, Shri 
Nariman embarked on a journey that would shap e the course of legal history in India.  
Throughout his illustrious career spanni ng over seven decades, Shri Nariman 
distinguished himself as a fo rmidable advocate, known for his incisive arguments and 
profound understanding of the law.   
 He resigned from the post of Addition al Solicitor General in 1975 in protest 
against the imposition of Emergency.  In his autobiography 'Before Memory Fades' he 
reflects, I quote, "One of the lessons of the Internal Emergency (of June, 1975) was 
2
[RAJYA SABHA]

not to rely on constitutional functionaries.  These functionaries failed us--Ministers of 
Government, Members of Parliament, Judg es of the Supreme Court, even the 
President of India.  It was because the Presid ent of India so readily agreed to sign the 
Proclamation of Emergency on the night of 25-6-1975, even before the Cabinet 
(Council of Ministers) knew anything about it." 
 For his distinguished services in the fiel d of jurisprudence, Shri Fali S. Nariman 
was conferred several prestigious awards , notable being Padma Bhushan in 1991, 
International Justice Prize in 2002 and Padma Vibhushan in 2007 among others. 
 In the passing away of Shri Fali S.  Nariman, the country has lost a shining 
beacon of jurisprudence, a champion of civil liberties and constitutional rights and an 
able parliamentarian. 
  Shri Sushil Kumar Modi passed away on the 13
th of May, 2024.  He served as a 
Member of this House representing the Stat e of Bihar from December, 2020 to April, 
2024.  Shri Sushil Kumar Modi was deeply committed to development of the State of 
Bihar and nation at large. He served as a Member of the Bihar Legislature for three 
terms between 1990 to 2020 and held the offi ce of Deputy Chief Minister, Minister of 
Parliamentary Affairs and Finance and Leader of Opposition in his long career. He was 
also a Member of the 14
th Lok Sabha.  In the passing away of Shri Sushil Kumar Modi, 
the country has lost an able administrato r, a committed leader and an experienced 
legislator.  
 Shri Murlidhar Chandrakant Bhandare passed away on the 15
th of June, 2024.  
He served as a Member of this House repr esenting the State of Maharashtra for three 
consecutive terms from June, 1980 to April, 1994.   
 Shri Bhandare also served as the Govern or of the State of Odisha from 2007 to 
2013.  As senior advocate, Shri Bhandare  was the President of the Supreme Court 
Bar Association from 1986 to 1988.  He championed the cause of common man in his 
profession as well as in his capacity as parliamentarian.  
 In the passing away of Shri Murlid har Chandrakant Bhandare, the country has 
lost an eminent legal luminary, a champi on of the rights of common man and a 
distinguished parliamentarian.

situation arising out of...

OBSERVATIONS BY THE CHAIR 
 
MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon. Members, I have received 22 notices on the subject to discuss 
on the situation arising out of irregularities, cheating and paper leakage in the National 
Eligibility Cum Entrance Test (NEET), 2024, conducted by National Testing Agency 
(NTA).   
 Hon. Members, hon. Rashtrapati ji, in her Address, delivered yesterday to the 
Members of both the Houses assemb led together, had in paragraph 20 inter alia  
indicated that there would be a fair investigation ensuring strict punishment to the 
culprits and that the Government is work ing towards major reforms in examination-
related bodies, their functioning and a ll aspects of the examination process. ... 
(Interruptions)... One minute, please take your seat .  Would you care to read para 
20? It is not good to rise suddenly. I ha ve gone through it. Read para 20, please. 
There is an express reflection therein. Hon. Members, the discussion on the Motion of 
Thanks on the hon. President's Address will be initiated in the House today. Thus, all 
hon. Members will have ample adequate oppo rtunity to put forth their views in the 
matter. Under these circumstances, I am constrained not to accept these notices 
under Rule 267.

SHRI GOLLA BABURAO (Andhra Pradesh) :  Thank you, Sir.  I am a newly-elected 
Member from Andhra Pradesh. First of all,  I want to introduce myself. My name is 
Golla Baburao. I have been sent to Rajya Sabha with the blessings of our former Chief 
Minister, Shri Jagan Mohan Reddy .  In fact, I am the first dalit  Member from Andhra 
Pradesh who has been sent to Rajya Sabha. I really feel very proud and on behalf of 
the entire dalit samaj , I thank our former Chief Minister, Shri Jagan Mohan Reddy.  
Before I respond to the President’s Speech made on 27
th June, I want to congratulate 
this House for the fact that Shrimati Dr oupadi Murmu is a tribal woman who has 
occupied the position of the President of India.  I congratulate the BJP Government 
and the entire Indian voters for selecting Shrimati Droupadi Murmu as the President of 
our nation.  At the same time, I want to congratulate the hon. Prime Minister for two 
to three important decisions he has taken in these ten years.  He is now a great 
statesman of the country because he has enacted the Women’s Reservation Bill in the 
Parliament and reserved 33 per cent of seats for women in the Lok Sabha and State 
Legislatures.  In fact, Dr. B.R. Ambedk ar demanded women equality in the Hindu 
Code Bill in 1950s, but because of certain problems, that Bill could not be passed. 
But that desire was fulfilled by Shri Modi and the BJP Government.  So many 
achievements are there.  There have been marvellous developments in all spheres of 
life in the country.  He abolished Article 370 and made Kashmir a Union Territory.  I 
am proud to say that I was a senior Gove rnment Officer in Andhra Pradesh for 25

years.  I was a three-time MLA in Andh ra Pradesh Assembly.  Through all these

important things.  These are industrial sect or, start-up sector, agriculture sector and

Kindly take important measures for the social sector. I have read the Address given by

This is also a very important point to recall.  My request to the BJP Government is that 
there are 60 per cent poor peop le living in India.  Your Viksit Bharat  will become a 
success only when you take measures for the social sector, for the development of 
the poor people.

almost all the States.

been neglected since time immemorial.  So me new schemes should also be brought 
for the betterment of women in the entire country.

nd June, 2014.  Just before dividing the State into two States, namely Telangana

also, the newly-formed Government is of  the NDA.  Our Chief Minister, Shri 
Chandrababu Naidu garu, is now a partner in the Union NDA Government.

3.00 P.M.

Demand for a probe into post poll violence against persons belonging to SC and ST in 
West Bengal after the recently held general elections 
 
SHRI SAMIK BHATTACHARYA (West Bengal) :  Madam, unspeakable atrocities on 
people belonging to the Scheduled Ca ste and Scheduled Tribe Categories and 
minority community post declaration of results of the General Election to the House of 
the People are taking place in West Bengal.  Right from the beginning of campaigning 
for the 18th Lok Sabha elections in West Be ngal and to the declaration of the results 
of the General Election to the House of th e People, 2024, unspeakable atrocities have 
been unleashed on the poor people, includ ing women and children belonging to the 
Scheduled Caste and Scheduled Tribe cate gories rendering many of them dead, 
maimed and homeless. A total of eight innocent people have lost  their lives because 
of this mindless attack. 
 I would, therefore, like to request the Government to undertake concrete 
action to protect the lives and properties of persons belonging to the Scheduled 
Caste and Scheduled Tribe categories affected by the post-poll violence in West 
Bengal. 
 
[ 28 June, 2024 ]

Mandatory self-declaration certificates for advertisements 
 
SHRI KARTIKEYA SHARMA (Haryana) :  Madam, thank you for allowing me to 
address this critical issue affecting print and digital media.  The recent directive 
requiring self-declaration certificates for all advertisements (print, digital and 
electronic) aims to safeguard consumer inte rests and uphold advertisement integrity.  
However, this directive has introduced several operational challenges.   
 There remains significant ambiguit y, especially regarding non-claim 
advertisements published by small media houses for SMEs. These advertisers may 
struggle to comply due to the technical nature of the process and limited resources. 
Additionally, the handling of Government and Public Sector advertisements under this 
directive is unclear.  Media houses face difficulties in preparing self-declaration 
certificates, registering on the portal, and troubleshooting issues, which could deter 
advertisers from using print media and affect revenue.  
 Storage of self-declarations, weed ing out, and handling emergency 
advertisements pose further challenges, particularly within the digital media 
ecosystem. Given the potential for legal challenges and technical issues with the 
portal, it is requested that the Ministry delay the implementation of these guidelines 
until clear and objective procedures are esta blished. It is also suggested that the 
implementation be initially limited to medi cal ads and that a wider consultation with 
stakeholders be conducted.  Thank you. 
 
THE VICE-CHAIRPERSON (SHRIMATI MEDHA VISHRAM KULKARNI):  With this, the 
House stands adjourned to meet at 11.00 a.m. on Monday, the 1
st July, 2024.
